\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{B}}[\widehat{H}_{\mathfrak{B}}](\theta):=\big(\hat{\psi}_{q}(\theta),\widehat{H}_{\mathfrak{B}}(\theta)\hat{\psi}_{p}(\theta)\big)_{\mathscr{F}_{\theta}}

(3.28)

the last equality in (
3.27
) allows us to view the infinite matrix (
3.26
) as associated with the following operator acting in
\ell^{2}(\Gamma)\otimes{\mathbb{C}}^{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}
:

\widetilde{\mathfrak{Op}}\big(\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{B}}[\widehat{H}_{\mathfrak{B}}]\big)\,:=\,\underset{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\sum}\mathring{U}(-\gamma)\otimes\Big[\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}_{*}}\big(\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{B}}[\widehat{H}_{\mathfrak{B}}](\theta)\big)\Big](\gamma)\,\in\,\mathbb{B}\big(\ell^{2}(\Gamma)\big)\otimes_{H}\mathscr{M}(n_{\mathfrak{B}})\,,

(3.29)

where
\otimes_{H}
is the so-called
spatial tensor product
of the two
C^{*}
-algebras associated with their canonical representations on the Hilbert spaces
\ell^{2}(\Gamma)
and
{\mathbb{C}}^{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}
.

One may compare the formula (
3.29
) with the Weyl quantization of a
\Gamma_{*}
-periodic symbol that does not depend on the configuration variables
x\in\mathcal{X}
:

\mathfrak{Op}(F)=\int_{\mathcal{X}}dz\big(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{X}^{*}}F\big)(z)U(-z)=\underset{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\sum}\big(\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}_{*}}F\big)(\gamma)U(-\gamma).

(3.30)

In conclusion, we may view
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}[H_{\mathfrak{B}}]
as a quantization of
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{B}}[\widehat{H}_{\mathfrak{B}}]\circ\mathfrak{p}_{*}:\mathcal{X}^{*}\rightarrow\mathscr{M}(n_{\mathfrak{B}})
through a Weyl calculus on the lattice
\Gamma
with matrix valued symbols.

3.5
Weyl symbols for the non perturbed decomposition

We close this section with an analysis of the symbols of some
\mathfrak{B}
-operators considered as pseudo-differential operators. We shall start from their distribution kernels defined via the Parseval frame (
3.23
).

Proposition 3.12
.

There exists
p_{\mathfrak{B}}
in
S^{-\infty}(\Xi)_{\Gamma}
such that
P_{\mathfrak{B}}\equiv\mathfrak{Op}(p_{\mathfrak{B}})
.

Proof.

In order to obtain the explicit relation between the
\Gamma
-periodic tempered distribution
p_{\mathfrak{B}}
(such that
P_{\mathfrak{B}}\equiv\mathfrak{Op}(p_{\mathfrak{B}})
) and the integral kernel
\mathfrak{K}[P_{\mathfrak{B}}]
(in the above Corollary
3.9
) we notice that:

\displaystyle P_{\mathfrak{B}}=\mathfrak{Op}(p_{\mathfrak{B}})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\,\mathfrak{K}[P_{\mathfrak{B}}]

and for
A=0
, Equation (
B.2
) and the formula of
\Upsilon\in\mathbb{B}(\mathbb{R}^{2d})
in Remark
B.2
imply that:

\displaystyle p_{\mathfrak{B}}(z,\zeta)=\big({\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{X}}\big)\big(\mathfrak{K}[P_{\mathfrak{B}}])\circ\Upsilon^{-1}\big)(z,\zeta)=

(3.31)

\displaystyle=\hskip-5.69046pt\int_{\mathcal{X}}\hskip-5.69046ptdv\,e^{i<\zeta,v>}\,\Big(\underset{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\sum}\,\underset{p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\big(\tau_{-\gamma}\psi_{p}(z+v/2)\big)\,\overline{\big(\tau_{-\gamma}\psi_{p}(z-v/2)\big)}\Big).

∎

The definitions given in Subsection
3.1
imply the equality
H_{\mathfrak{B}}=P_{\mathfrak{B}}H^{\circ}P_{\mathfrak{B}}
, and the fact that
H_{\mathfrak{B}}
has a symbol
h_{\mathfrak{B}}
in
S^{-\infty}(\Xi)_{\Gamma}
,
H_{\bot}:=H^{\circ}-H_{\mathfrak{B}}
has a symbol
h_{\bot}\in{S}^{p}_{1}(\Xi)_{\Gamma}
and we have the formulas:

H_{\mathfrak{B}}=\mathfrak{Op}(h_{\mathfrak{B}}),\quad\,h_{\mathfrak{B}}=p_{\mathfrak{B}}\sharp{h}^{\circ}\sharp{p}_{\mathfrak{B}},\quad\,H_{\bot}=\mathfrak{Op}(h_{\bot}),\quad\,h_{\bot}:=h^{\circ}-h_{\mathfrak{B}}\,.

(3.32)

Due to Hypothesis
2.9
(that amounts to a simple shift of the energy origin) the interval
[0,E_{0}]\subset\mathbb{R}
is always non-void and it is a spectral gap for

H_{\bot}
. In what follows, we will need the notation introduced in (
3.1
), (
3.2
), (
3.3
) and (
3.4
).

The operator
P_{\mathfrak{B}}
coincides with the orthogonal projection
P_{\ker H_{\bot}}
on the kernel of
H_{\bot}
.
Since the interval
(E_{-},E_{+})
is a spectral gap for
H_{\bot}
, we consider a contour
\mathscr{C}_{0}
which does not contain
0
in its interior but contains the interval
[E_{0},(E_{-}+E_{+})/2]
, and stays at a positive distance from the spectrum of
H_{\bot}
. Then we have the usual formulas:

\big(\mathfrak{Op}(h_{\bot})-\mathcal{z}{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}\big)^{-1}=\mathfrak{Op}\big((h_{\bot}-\mathcal{z})^{-}\big),\quad{p}_{0}=-(2\pi i)^{-1}\int_{\mathscr{C}_{0}}d\mathcal{z}\,\big(h_{\bot}-\mathcal{z}\big)^{-},\quad\,h_{0}=p_{0}\sharp{h^{\circ}}\sharp{p}_{0}\,,

(3.33)

implying that
P_{0}
and
H_{0}
have symbols of class
S^{-\infty}(\Xi)_{\Gamma}
.

Finally we also have the equalities

p_{\infty}=1-p_{0}-p_{\mathfrak{B}}\mbox{ and }h_{\infty}=p_{\infty}\sharp{h}^{\circ}\sharp{p}_{\infty}=1-h_{0}-h_{\mathfrak{B}}\,,

(3.34)

implying that
H_{\infty}=H^{\circ}-H_{\mathfrak{B}}-H_{0}
and
P_{\infty}
have symbols
h_{\infty}
in
S^{p}_{1}(\Xi)_{\Gamma}
and
p_{\infty}
in
S^{0}_{1}(\Xi)_{\Gamma}
.

4
The perturbed decomposition induced by
\mathfrak{B}
.

Our main purpose now is to use some kind of local gauge choices inspired from
[
49
]
and define a family of “modified magnetic translations” (see also
[
67
]
) generalizing the
Zak translations
(from Definition
6.1
), in order to obtain a “magnetic” version of the family
\text{\large{$\boldsymbol{\Psi}$}}_{\mathfrak{B}}
in (
3.23
) and through it the desired projection
P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
in Theorem
2.18
as a kind of
\epsilon
-dependent “magnetic version” of the orthogonal projection
P_{\mathfrak{B}}
. An important technical difficulty is to elaborate a procedure replacing the usual Gram-Schmidt orthonormalization method in the case of frames. We begin with some preliminary technical results concerning the decomposition of the Hamiltonian
H^{\epsilon}
extending that of
H^{\circ}
.

4.1
The perturbed Hamiltonian decomposition

The symbol decomposition
h^{\circ}=h_{0}+h_{\mathfrak{B}}+h_{\infty}
discussed above and the properties of magnetic quantization allow us to consider a similar operator decomposition when the perturbing magnetic field given in (
2.10
) is added (we use the notations in (
2.11
)):

\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}(h^{\circ})\,=\,\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}(h_{0})+\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}(h_{\mathfrak{B}})+\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}(h_{\infty}).

(4.1)

We shall use the notations
H^{\epsilon}_{0}\equiv\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}(h_{0})
,
H^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\equiv\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}(h_{\mathfrak{B}})
and
H^{\epsilon}_{\infty}
for the self-adjoint closure of
\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}(h_{\infty})
.
We also consider the lower-semibounded self-adjoint operator

H^{\epsilon}_{\bot}:=H_{0}^{\epsilon}+H^{\epsilon}_{\infty}

equal to the closure of
\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}(h_{\bot})
with symbol
h_{\bot}\in S^{p}_{1}(\Xi)_{\Gamma}
given in (
3.32
).

An important aspect is that these perturbed Hamiltonians no longer commute among themselves, as it was the case in Subsection
3.1
. Another important observation, based on the results in
[
2
,
24
]
is that their spectra, considered as subsets of
\mathbb{R}
, do not change much as subsets of
\mathbb{R}
and we have the following result.

Remark 4.1
.

Under the hypothesis of Theorem
2.18
, there exists
\delta_{0}>0
and for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}]
there exists
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that for any
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
we have the inclusions:

\begin{split}&\sigma\big(H_{0}^{\epsilon}\big)\subset[-\delta\,,\,\delta]\cup[E_{0}-\delta,E_{-}+\delta],\quad\sigma\big(H_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}\big)\subset[-\delta\,,\,\delta]\cup[E^{\prime}_{-}-\delta,E^{\prime}_{+}+\delta],\\
&\sigma\big(H^{\epsilon}_{\infty}\big)\subset[-\delta\,,\,\delta]\cup[E_{+}-\delta,+\infty),\\
&\sigma\big(H^{\epsilon}_{\bot}\big)\subset[-\delta\,,\,\delta]\cup[E_{0}-\delta,E_{-}+\delta]\cup[E_{+}-\delta,+\infty).\end{split}

(4.2)

Actually, much more precise regularity estimates for the spectral edges seen as functions of
\epsilon
were obtained in
[
25
]
, but they are not needed here.

4.2
Magnetic perturbation of the Parseval frame.

Recalling (
2.11
), we introduce the following notations:

\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\gamma}(x):=\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(x,\gamma),\quad\forall\gamma\in\Gamma,\qquad\widetilde{\Omega}^{\epsilon}(x,y,z):=\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(x,y)\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(y,z)\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(z,x).

(4.3)

4.2.1
The inhomogeneous Zak translations and the perturbed Parseval frame

Guided by the same intuition as for the construction of the modified Wannier functions in
[
57
,
12
]
and by the analysis in
[
21
]
and using the notations (
4.3
), let us introduce the following system of functions:

\widetilde{\text{\large{$\boldsymbol{\Psi}$}}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}:=\big\{\widetilde{\psi}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma,p}:=\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\gamma}\big(\tau_{-\gamma}\psi_{p}\big)\equiv\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma}(\psi_{p}),\,\gamma\in\Gamma,\,p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}\big\}\in L^{2}(\mathcal{X})

(4.4)

and let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
be the closed subspace that they generate.

Having in mind the arguments and constructions in Subsection 3.4 in
[
19
]
, let us consider for any
N\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}
the following integral kernels of finite-rank integral operators (with the notations defined after (
2.2
)):

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},N}(x,y)\,:=\,\hskip-11.38092pt\underset{|\gamma|\leq\,N,\,p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\hskip-8.5359pt\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma}\psi_{p}(x)\,\overline{\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma}\psi_{p}(y)}\,\equiv\,\underset{|\gamma|\leq\,N}{\sum}\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},\gamma}(x,y)

(4.5)

and notice that
\big[\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big]^{\bot}\subset\mathbb{K}{\rm er}\,\big[\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\,\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},N}\big]
and
\mathbb{R}{\rm ge}\,\big[\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\,\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},N}\big]\subset\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
for any
N\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}
.

Lemma 4.2
.

With the notations in (
4.3
), given any
n\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
C_{n}>0
such that for any
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
and any
(\gamma,p)\in\Gamma\times\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}
, we have:

\forall(\hat{x},\hat{y})\in\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{E}:\quad\big|\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma}\psi_{p}(\hat{x}+\alpha)\,\overline{\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma}\psi_{p}(\hat{y}+\beta)}\big|\,\leq\,C_{n}<\alpha-\beta>^{-n},\qquad\forall(\alpha,\beta)\in\Gamma\times\Gamma.

Proof.

Using (
3.21
) and (
3.22
) we can write:

\displaystyle\big|\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma}\psi_{p}(\hat{x}+\alpha)\,\overline{\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma}\psi_{p}(\hat{y}+\beta)}\big|=

\displaystyle\ =(2\pi)^{-2d}\Big|\Lambda^{\epsilon}(\hat{x}+\alpha,\gamma)\overline{\Lambda^{\epsilon}(\hat{y}+\beta,\gamma)}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{T}_{*}}d{\theta}\,e^{i<{\theta},\alpha-\gamma>}\,\hat{\psi}_{p}(\hat{x};{\theta})\Big)\Big(\int_{\mathbb{T}_{*}}d{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{z}}}^{*}}\,e^{-i<{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{z}}}^{*}},\beta-\gamma>}\,\overline{\hat{\psi}_{p}(\hat{y};{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{z}}}^{*}})}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\,C^{\prime}_{n}<\alpha-\gamma>^{-n}<\beta-\gamma>^{-n}\big(\underset{\theta\in\mathbb{T}_{*}}{\sup}\,\underset{\hat{x}\in\mathcal{E}}{\sup}\,\big|\big(\partial_{\theta}^{n}\hat{\psi}_{p}\big)(\hat{x};\theta)\big|\big)^{2}\,\leq\,{C}_{n}<\alpha-\beta>^{-n}.

∎

Proposition 4.3
.

For any
N\in\mathbb{N}
, the integral kernel
\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},N}
has rapid off-diagonal decay and defines a bounded self-adjoint integral operator.

In order to control the limit for
N\nearrow\infty
we shall use the Cotlar-Stein Lemma (see for example Lemma 18.6.5 in
[
39
]
) and thus we need to estimate the products:

\begin{split}\big[\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\big(\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},\alpha}\big)\big]^{*}\cdot\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\big(\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},\beta}\big)\,&=\,\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\big[\big(\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},\alpha}\big)^{\dagger}\diamond\big(\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},\beta}\big)\big]\,,\\
\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\big(\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},\alpha}\big)\cdot\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\big[\big(\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},\beta}\big)\big]^{*}\,&=\,\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\big[\big(\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},\alpha}\big)\diamond\big(\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},\beta}\big)^{\dagger}\big]\,.\end{split}

\displaystyle\big[\big(\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},\alpha}\big)^{\dagger}\diamond\big(\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},\beta}\big)\big]=\big[\big(\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},\alpha}\big)\diamond\big(\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},\beta}\big)\big]=\big[\big(\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},\alpha}\big)\diamond\big(\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},\beta}\big)^{\dagger}\big]\,;

\displaystyle\big[\big(\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},\alpha}\big)\diamond\big(\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},\beta}\big)^{\dagger}\big](x,y)=

\displaystyle\quad=\underset{p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\ \underset{q\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\int_{\mathcal{X}}dz\,\Big(\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\psi_{p}(x)\,\overline{(\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\psi_{p})(z)}\Big((\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\psi)_{q}(z)\Big)\,\overline{\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\psi_{q}(y)}\Big)\,.

The following computations prove that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
there exists a constant
C_{n}>0
such that, for any
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
, we have:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}dz\,\overline{(\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\psi_{p})(z)}\,(\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\psi)_{q}(z)=\int_{\mathcal{X}}dz\,\overline{\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\psi_{p}(z-\alpha)}\,\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\psi_{q}(z-\beta)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathcal{X}}dz\,\Big(<z-\alpha>^{n}\overline{\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\psi_{p}(z-\alpha)}\Big)\,\Big(<z-\beta>^{n}\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\psi_{q}(z-\beta)\Big)<z-\alpha>^{-n}<z-\beta>^{-n}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\,C_{n}<\alpha-\beta>^{-n}\underset{p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\max}[{\nu}_{n+(d+1)/2,0}(\psi_{p})]^{2}.

Via the Schur test and Proposition
4.3
, the sums over
(p,q)
being finite, the above estimation gives the necessary condition for applying the Cotlar-Stein Lemma and obtaining the following result.

Proposition 4.4
.

The limit
\underset{N\nearrow\infty}{\lim}\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\,\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},N}\,=:\,\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\in\mathbb{B}\big(L^{2}(\mathcal{X})\big)
exists in the strong operator topology on
\mathbb{B}\big(L^{2}(\mathcal{X})\big)
. Moreover, the limit
\underset{N\nearrow\infty}{\lim}\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},N}=:\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
also exists uniformly on compact sets in
BC^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})
, it belongs to
\mathring{\mathscr{S}}_{\Delta}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})
(see Notation
2.1
) and
\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
equals the distribution kernel of
\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
.

An important technical difficulty is that in general the operator
\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
is not the orthogonal projection on
\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
. In fact we have the following result.

Proposition 4.5
.

The operator
\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}:=\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\,\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
is self-adjoint and satisfies the following identity (in
\mathbb{B}\big(L^{2}(\mathcal{X})\big)
):

[\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]^{2}=\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,+\,\mathscr{O}(\epsilon).

Proof.

Let us fix some
N\in\mathbb{N}
and compute:

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\big[(\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},N})\diamond(\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},N})\big](x,y)=

(4.6)

\displaystyle=\underset{|\alpha|\leq\,N,\,p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\ \underset{|\beta|\leq\,N,\,q\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\int_{\mathcal{X}}dz\,\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\psi_{p}(x)\,\overline{\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\psi_{p}(z)}\,\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\psi_{q}(z)\,\overline{\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\psi_{q}(y)}.

Let us make a deeper study of the
z
-integral appearing in the above sums, following the analysis in Appendix
B.3.1
:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}dz\,\overline{\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\psi_{p}(z)}\,\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\psi_{q}(z)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{X}}dz\,\overline{\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}(z)\psi_{p}(z-\alpha)}\,\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(z)\psi_{q}(z-\beta)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\int_{\mathcal{X}}dz\,\widetilde{\Omega}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,z,\beta)\overline{\psi_{p}(z-\alpha)}\,\psi_{q}(z-\beta)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\big((\tau_{-\alpha}\psi_{p})\,,\,{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}\,(\tau_{-\beta}\psi_{q})\big)_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}\,+\,\epsilon\,\big[[\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},1}]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\big[\mathfrak{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}[{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}\,+\,\epsilon\,\big[[\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},1}]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}\,,

(4.7)

where we used (
3.25
) for the main term and put into evidence the remainder:

\displaystyle\big[[\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},1}]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}:=

(4.8)

\displaystyle\ =-i\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\int_{\mathcal{X}}dz\,\Big(\int_{<\alpha,z,\beta>}\,B^{\epsilon}\Big)\,\int_{0}^{1}ds\exp\Big(-is\epsilon\int_{<\alpha,z,\beta>}\,B^{\epsilon}\Big)\overline{\psi_{p}(z-\alpha)}\,\psi_{q}(z-\beta),

with the following estimation implied by the arguments in Appendix
B.3.1
(making use of Notation
2.13
):

\begin{split}&\forall N\in\mathbb{N},\ \exists\,C_{N}(\mathbf{B})>0:\quad\big|\big[[\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},1}]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}\big|\leq\\
&\ \leq\,{C_{N}(\mathbf{B})}\,<\alpha-\beta>^{-N}\,\big[\epsilon\,\big(\underset{p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\max}{\nu}_{N+d+2,0}(\psi_{p})^{2}\big)\,+\,\mathscr{O}(\epsilon^{2})\big],\ \forall(p,q)\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}\times\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}},\ \forall\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}].\end{split}

When inserting into (
4.6
) the first term in (
4.7
), we notice that:

\displaystyle\underset{|\alpha|\leq\,N,\,p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\ \underset{|\beta|\leq\,N,\,q\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\hskip-8.5359pt\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\big[\mathfrak{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}[{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\psi_{p}\otimes\overline{\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\psi_{q}}=

\displaystyle\quad=\underset{|\alpha|\leq\,N,\,p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\ \underset{|\beta|\leq\,N,\,q\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\hskip-8.5359pt\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}(x)\,[\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(y)^{-1}]\big[\mathfrak{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}[{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}\psi_{p}(x-\alpha)\,\overline{\psi_{q}(y-\beta)}

\displaystyle=\underset{|\alpha|\leq\,N,\,p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\ \underset{|\beta|\leq\,N,\,q\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\hskip-8.5359pt\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(x,y))\widetilde{\Omega}^{\epsilon}(\beta,x,\alpha)\,\widetilde{\Omega}^{\epsilon}(x,\beta,y)\Big[\big[\mathfrak{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}[{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}[\tau_{\alpha}\psi_{p}]\otimes\overline{[\tau_{\beta}\psi_{q}]}\Big](x,y)

\displaystyle\quad=\underset{|\alpha|\leq\,N,\,p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\ \underset{|\beta|\leq\,N,\,q\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\hskip-8.5359pt\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(x,y))\Big\{\Big[\big[\mathfrak{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}[{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}[\tau_{\alpha}\psi_{p}]\otimes\overline{[\tau_{\beta}\psi_{q}]}\,+\,\epsilon\,\big[[\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},2}]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}\Big](x,y)\Big\}

with
\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},2}
a remainder similar with
\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},1}
.

For the main term above, Corollary
3.9
implies the formulas

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}\underset{|\alpha|\leq\,N_{1}}{\sum}\,\underset{p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\Big[\underset{|\beta|\leq\,N_{2}}{\sum}\,\underset{q\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\Big(\big[\mathfrak{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}[{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}[\tau_{\alpha}\psi_{p}]\otimes\overline{[\tau_{\beta}\psi_{q}]}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad=\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}\underset{|\alpha|\leq\,N_{1}}{\sum}\,\underset{p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}[\tau_{\alpha}\psi_{p}]\,\otimes\,\Big[\underset{|\beta|\leq\,N_{2}}{\sum}\,\underset{q\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\big([\tau_{\alpha}\psi_{p}]\,,\,[\tau_{\beta}\psi_{q}]\big)_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}\overline{[\tau_{\beta}\psi_{q}]}\Big]

\displaystyle\ \underset{N_{2}\nearrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\ \widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}\underset{|\alpha|\leq\,N_{1}}{\sum}\,\underset{p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}[\tau_{\alpha}\psi_{p}]\otimes\overline{[\tau_{\alpha}\psi_{p}]}\ \underset{N_{1}\nearrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\ \mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}\mathfrak{K}[P_{\mathfrak{B}}].

Finally let us notice that:

\displaystyle[\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}\mathfrak{K}[P_{\mathfrak{B}}]\,-\,\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}](x,y)\,=\,\hskip-11.38092pt\underset{\alpha\in\Gamma,\,p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\hskip-8.5359pt\Big[\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(x,y)[\tau_{\alpha}\psi_{p}(x)]\,\overline{[\tau_{\alpha}\psi_{p}(y)]}\,-\,\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\psi_{p}(x)\,\overline{\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\psi_{p}(y)}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad=\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(x,y)\hskip-11.38092pt\underset{\alpha\in\Gamma,\,p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\hskip-8.5359pt\big(1\,-\,\widetilde{\Omega}^{\epsilon}(x,\alpha,y)\big)[\tau_{\alpha}\psi_{p}(x)]\,\overline{[\tau_{\alpha}\psi_{p}(y)]}\,,

in order to conclude that the arguments in Appendix
B.3.1
imply the estimation:

\begin{split}\forall N\in\mathbb{N},\ \exists\,{C_{N}(\mathbf{B})}>0:\ &\big|[\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}\mathfrak{K}[P_{\mathfrak{B}}]\,-\,\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}](x,y)\big|\leq\\
&\leq\,{C_{N}(\mathbf{B})}\,<x-y>^{-N}\,\big[\epsilon\,\big(\underset{p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\max}{\nu}_{N+d+2,0}(\psi_{p})^{2}\big)\,+\,\mathscr{O}(\epsilon^{2})\big],\ \forall\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}].\end{split}

For the second term in (
4.7
) we have to study the limit:

\displaystyle\underset{N\nearrow\infty}{\lim}\underset{|\alpha|\leq\,N,\,|\beta|\leq\,N}{\sum}\ \underset{(p,q)\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}^{2}}{\sum}\hskip-8.5359pt\big[[\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}\,\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\psi_{p}(x)\,\overline{\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\psi_{q}(y)}\,\equiv\,\underset{N\nearrow\infty}{\lim}\underset{|\alpha|\leq\,N,\,|\beta|\leq\,N}{\sum}\,\tilde{\mathfrak{K}}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha,\beta}(x,y)\,,

and we use once again the Cotlar Stein Lemma with the countable family of indices
\Gamma\times\Gamma
. In fact we notice that we have:

\displaystyle\big[\tilde{\mathfrak{K}}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha,\beta}\diamond\tilde{\mathfrak{K}}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}\big](x,y)=

\displaystyle\quad=\hskip-8.5359pt\underset{(p,q)\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}^{2}}{\sum}\ \underset{(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}^{2}}{\sum}\hskip-8.5359pt\big[[\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}\big[[\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]_{\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}\big]_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}\Big[\delta_{\alpha^{\prime},\beta}\delta_{p^{\prime},q}+\epsilon\,\big[[\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]_{\alpha^{\prime},\beta}\big]_{p^{\prime},q}\Big]\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\psi_{p}(x)\,\overline{\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\beta^{\prime}}\psi_{q^{\prime}}(y)}\,,

and thus, for any
(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
there exists
C>0
such that:

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\big[\tilde{\mathfrak{K}}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha,\beta}\diamond\tilde{\mathfrak{K}}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}\big]\Big\|_{\mathbb{B}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))}\,

\displaystyle\leq\,C<\alpha-\beta>^{-m_{1}}<\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}>^{-m_{2}}\big[\delta_{\alpha^{\prime},\beta}+\epsilon<\alpha^{\prime}-\beta>^{-m_{3}}\big]

and choosing asequately the powers
(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})
one may obtain that for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
C_{N}>0
such that:

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\big[\tilde{\mathfrak{K}}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha,\beta}\diamond\tilde{\mathfrak{K}}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}\big]\Big\|_{\mathbb{B}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))}\,

\displaystyle\leq\,C_{N}<\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}>^{-N}<\beta-\beta^{\prime}>^{-N}.

∎

Corollary 4.6
.

For some
\epsilon_{0}>0
as stipulated in (
2.10
) there exists some
C_{Q}>0
such that for any
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
one has the inclusion:

\sigma(\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})\subset(-C_{Q}\epsilon,C_{Q}\epsilon)\bigcup(1-C_{Q}\epsilon,1+C_{Q}\epsilon).

Proof.

As
\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
is a bounded self-adjoint operator, Proposition
4.5
above implies that if
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
is in its spectrum, then it obeys
|\lambda^{2}-\lambda|\leq\,C_{Q}\epsilon
for some
C_{Q}>0
, that can be obtained from (
4.8
), and any
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
.
∎

We shall call
\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}

the ’quasi-projection’ associated with the frame

\widetilde{\text{\large{$\boldsymbol{\Psi}$}}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
.

4.2.2
The magnetic symbol of the “quasi-projection”

Proposition 4.7
.

There exists
\tilde{q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\in{S}^{-\infty}(\Xi)
such that

\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}=\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}(\tilde{q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}).

Proof.

Proposition
4.4
implies that
\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}=\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\,\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}=\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}(\tilde{q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})
with

\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\in\mathring{\mathscr{S}}_{\Delta}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})
(as in Notation
2.1
).
Having in view Formula (
B.2
) from the appendices, the equalities

\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}=\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\,\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}=\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}(\tilde{q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})

imply that:

\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}\,\big[\big(({\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{X}^{*}})\tilde{q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big)\circ\Upsilon\big]\,=\,\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}},\quad\text{i.e.:}\quad\tilde{q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}=\big({\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{X}}\big)\big[[(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon})^{-1}\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]\circ\Upsilon^{-1}\big]\,.

(4.9)

We know that:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\underset{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\sum}\ \underset{p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\big[\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma}\,\psi_{p}\big](x)\,\overline{\big[\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma}\,\psi_{p}\big](y)}\,,

and conclude that:

\displaystyle\tilde{q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}(z,\zeta)=

(4.10)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathcal{X}}dv\,e^{i<\zeta,v>}\Big[\underset{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\sum}\ \underset{p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\Omega^{\epsilon}(z+v/2,\gamma,z-v/2)\,\big[\tau_{-\gamma}\psi_{p}\big](z+v/2)\,\overline{\big[\tau_{-\gamma}\psi_{p}\big](z-v/2)}\Big]\,.

∎

Lemma 4.8
.

With the above notations and hypothesis, for any continuous semi-norm
{\nu}:S^{-\infty}(\Xi)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
there exists some constant
C_{\nu}>0
, such that:

{\nu}\big(\widetilde{q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,-\,p_{\mathfrak{B}}\big)\,\leq\,C_{\nu}\,\epsilon,\hskip 11.74988pt\forall\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}].

Proof.

We go back to the formulas (
4.9
) - (
4.10
) and (
3.31
) in order to make explicit the difference of symbols we want to estimate and obtain terms of the form:

\displaystyle\blacklozenge\hskip 28.45274pt

\displaystyle<\zeta>^{2n}\big(\widetilde{q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,-\,p_{\mathfrak{B}}\big)\big](z,\zeta)=<\zeta>^{2n}\int_{\mathcal{X}}dv\,e^{i<\zeta,v>}\,\times

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times\underset{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\sum}\ \underset{p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\big[\Omega^{\epsilon}(z+v/2,\gamma,z-v/2)-1\big]\,\big[\tau_{-\gamma}\psi_{p}\big](z+v/2)\,\overline{\big[\tau_{-\gamma}\psi_{p}\big](z-v/2)}

\displaystyle=(-i\epsilon)\int_{\mathcal{X}}dv\,e^{i<\zeta,v>}<i\partial_{v}>^{2n}\Big[\big(\int_{<z+v/2,\gamma,z-v/2>}B^{\epsilon}\big)\,\times

(4.11)

\displaystyle\times\,\Big[\int_{0}^{1}ds\exp\Big(-is\epsilon\int_{<z+v/2,\gamma,z-v/2>}B^{\epsilon}\Big)\Big]\underset{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\sum}\ \underset{p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\big[\tau_{-\gamma}\psi_{p}\big](z+v/2)\,\overline{\big[\tau_{-\gamma}\psi_{p}\big](z-v/2)}\Big],

\displaystyle\blacklozenge\hskip 14.22636pt<\zeta>^{2n}\big[\partial_{z}^{a}\partial_{\zeta}^{b}\big(\widetilde{q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,-\,p_{\mathfrak{B}}\big)\big](z,\zeta)\,=\,<\zeta>^{n}\Big[\partial_{z}^{a}\partial_{\zeta}^{b}\int_{\mathcal{X}}dv\,e^{i<\zeta,v>}\,\times

(4.12)

\displaystyle\times\underset{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\sum}\ \underset{p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\big[\Omega^{\epsilon}(z+v/2,\gamma,z-v/2)-1\big]\,\big[\tau_{-\gamma}\psi_{p}\big](z+v/2)\,\overline{\big[\tau_{-\gamma}\psi_{p}\big](z-v/2)}\,\Big]

\displaystyle

\displaystyle=\hskip-5.69046pt\int_{\mathcal{X}}\hskip-5.69046ptdv\,e^{i<\zeta,v>}<i\partial_{v}>^{2n}\Big[(iv)^{b}\underset{a^{\prime}\leq{a}}{\sum}C^{a}_{a^{\prime}}\,\big[\big(\partial_{z}^{a^{\prime}}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\epsilon}\big)(z+v/2,\gamma,z-v/2)\big]\,\times

\displaystyle\times\,\big[\partial_{z}^{a-a^{\prime}}\big(\psi_{p}(z-\gamma+v/2)\,\overline{\psi_{p}(z-\gamma-v/2)}\big)\big]\Big],

and also

\displaystyle\big(\partial^{c}_{v}\partial_{z}^{a}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\epsilon}\big)(z+v/2,\gamma,z-v/2)=\partial^{c}_{v}\partial_{z}^{a}\Big[\exp\Big(-i\epsilon\int_{<z+v/2,\gamma,z-v/2>}B^{\epsilon}\Big)\Big].

(4.13)

(
B.20
) in Appendix
B.3.2
gives us an upper bound that together with the last lines in (
4.11
) and (
4.12
) imply the conclusion of the Lemma.
∎

4.2.3
The “magnetic” modified projection for the isolated Bloch family.

Definition 4.9
.

For
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
with
\epsilon_{0}>0
small enough and
C_{Q}>0
as in Corollary
4.6
, we may choose a circle
\mathscr{C}_{\epsilon}\subset{\mathbb{C}}
centred in
1
with a radius
2C_{Q}\epsilon<1/2
and define:

P_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}:=\frac{i}{2\pi}\oint_{\mathscr{C}_{\epsilon}}d\mathcal{z}\,\big(\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}-\mathcal{z}{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}\big)^{-1}

as the spectral projection of
\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}
on the interval
(1-C_{Q}\epsilon,1+C_{Q}\epsilon)
. Let us also introduce the notation:

\mathfrak{L}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}:=\mathbb{R}{\rm ge}\,\,P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}

and call it the “
magnetic modified space
” associated with the isolated Bloch family
\mathfrak{B}
.

Remark 4.10
.

Since
\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}
is a self-adjoint operator as in Corollary
4.6
while
P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
is its spectral projection on the spectral island close to
1\in{\mathbb{C}}
, it follows that
\mathfrak{L}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\subset\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
and
\|\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}-P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\|_{\mathbb{B}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))}\leq{C}\epsilon
for some constant
C>0
(that can be estimated using the constant
C_{Q}
in Corollary
4.6
) and any
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
.

Lemma 4.11
.

With the above notations, for any continuous semi-norm
{\nu}:S^{-\infty}(\Xi)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
there exists some constant
C_{\nu}>0
, depending on a finite number of derivatives of
B^{\epsilon}\in{{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}_{\text{\tt bd}}^{2}(\mathcal{X})
, such that:

{\nu}\big(\widetilde{q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}-p^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big)\,\leq\,C_{\nu}\,\epsilon,\hskip 11.74988pt\forall\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}].

Proof.

Let us notice the following identity:

(\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}-\mathcal{z}{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}})\big(\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}+(\mathcal{z}-1){\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}\big)=\big(\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}\big)^{2}-\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}+\mathcal{z}(1-\mathcal{z}){\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}

(4.14)

implying that

\displaystyle(\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}-\mathcal{z}{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\big(\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}+(\mathcal{z}-1){\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}\big)\Big(\big(\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}\big)^{2}-\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}+\mathcal{z}(\mathcal{z}-1){\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}\Big)^{-1}

\displaystyle=:\mathcal{z}^{-1}(1-\mathcal{z})^{-1}\,\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}+\Big(\big(\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}\big)^{2}-\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}\Big)\,F(\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon},\mathcal{z})

where the function
F
can easily be read off by expanding the above inverse. Using the magnetic Beals criterion and the arguments in section 6.2 of
[
42
]
, we have that
F(\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon},\mathcal{z})
is a magnetic pseudo-differential operator with a symbol in
S^{0}
. The operator
\big(\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}\big)^{2}-\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}
has a symbol in
S^{-\infty}
which is also proportional with
\epsilon
as can be seen from Proposition
4.5
. Thus
\Big(\big(\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}\big)^{2}-\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}\Big)\,F(\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon},\mathcal{z})
has a symbol in
S^{-\infty}
, uniformly in
\mathcal{z}
, and proportional with
\epsilon
.
The final step is to integrate with respect to
\mathcal{z}
.
∎

Remark 4.12
.

The above Lemma
4.11
and Proposition
4.7
imply that
P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}=\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}(p^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})
with
p^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\in{S}^{-\infty}(\Xi)
and for any continuous semi-norm
{\nu}:S^{-\infty}(\Xi)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
there exists some constant
C_{\nu}>0
such that:

{\nu}\big(p^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,-\,p_{\mathfrak{B}}\big)\,\leq\,C_{\nu}\,\epsilon,\hskip 11.74988pt\forall\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}].

4.2.4
Commutator with the Hamiltonian.

As remarked before the definition (
3.22
), the functions
\psi_{p}
for
p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}
belong to
\mathscr{S}(\mathcal{X})
and we conclude that the family
\big\{\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma}\psi_{p},\ \gamma\in\Gamma,\ p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}\big\}
is contained in
\mathcal{D}(H^{\epsilon})
for any
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
for some
\epsilon_{0}>0
as discussed in subsection
4.2
.
Taking into account that
h^{\circ}\in{S}^{p}_{1}(\Xi)_{\Gamma}
and
p^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\in{S}^{-\infty}(\Xi)
for any
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
with some
\epsilon_{0}>0
, let us compute:

\displaystyle H^{\epsilon}P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}-P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,H^{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}(h^{\circ}\sharp^{\epsilon}{p}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}-{p}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\sharp^{\epsilon}{h})=\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}(h^{\circ}\sharp{p}_{\mathfrak{B}}-{p}_{\mathfrak{B}}\sharp{h^{\circ}})+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon).

\mathfrak{Op}^{\circ}\big(h^{\circ}\sharp{p}_{\mathfrak{B}}-p_{\mathfrak{B}}\sharp{h}^{\circ}\big)=H^{\circ}\,P_{\mathfrak{B}}\,-\,p_{\mathfrak{B}}\,H^{\circ}\,=\,0

and conclude that:

\big[\,H^{\epsilon}\,,\,P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,\big]\,=\,\mathcal{O}(\epsilon).

(4.15)

Proposition 4.13
.

Let
H^{\circ}
in (
2.7
) satisfy Hypothesis
2.10
and consider a perturbation by a magnetic field satisfying Hypothesis
2.10
. Then there exists
\delta_{0}>0
so that the interval

J^{\delta}_{\mathfrak{B}}:=\big(E_{-}+{2}\delta\,,\,E_{+}-{2}\delta\big)

is not empty for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}]
and for any test function
\varphi\in\,C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R})
with
\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits\varphi\subset J^{\delta}_{\mathfrak{B}}
, there exist
\epsilon_{0}>0
and
C>0
such that for any
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
we have:

\big\|P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,\varphi\big(H^{\epsilon}\big)\,-\,\varphi\big(H^{\epsilon}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{B}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))}\,\leq\,C\,\epsilon.

Proof.

The hypothesis
J:=\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits\varphi\subset(E_{-}+2\delta,E_{+}-2\delta)
implies that:

E_{J}(H^{\circ})=E_{J}(H_{\bot})\oplus\,E_{J}(H_{\mathfrak{B}})=E_{J}(H_{\mathfrak{B}})\subset\,E_{\{0\}}(H_{\bot})=P_{\mathfrak{B}}.

(4.16)

If we use Proposition 6.33 in
[
42
]
and the notations:

\begin{split}&\varphi\big(H^{\epsilon}\big)\,=\,\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon,\mathcal{c}}\big(\varphi^{\epsilon}[h]\big),\quad\varphi^{\epsilon}[h]\in\,S^{-p}_{1}(\mathcal{X}^{*}\times\mathcal{X}),\\
&\varphi\big(H^{\circ}\big)\,=\,\mathfrak{Op}^{\circ}\big(\varphi^{\circ}[h]\big),\quad\varphi^{\circ}[h]\in\,S^{-p}_{1}(\mathcal{X}^{*}\times\mathcal{X})\,,\end{split}

we notice that (
4.16
) implies the equality:

P_{\mathfrak{B}}\,\varphi\big(H^{\circ}\big)\,=\,\varphi\big(H^{\circ}\big)\quad\text{i.e.:}\quad\,p_{\mathfrak{B}}\sharp^{B^{\circ}}\varphi^{\circ}[h]\,-\,\varphi^{\circ}[h]\,=\,0\,.

Thus, the usual estimation of the magnetic perturbation on symbols implies that:

\begin{split}\big\|P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,\varphi\big(H^{\epsilon}\big)\,-\,\varphi\big(H^{\epsilon}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{B}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))}\,=\,\mathscr{O}(\epsilon).\end{split}

∎

4.3
A Parseval frame for the magnetic modified space

Now we shall elaborate a procedure of transforming any ”quasi-Parseval” frame into a Parseval one, replacing the Gram-Schmidt orthogonalization procedure and aply it for the closed subspace
\mathfrak{L}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}=P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}L^{2}(\mathcal{X})
with the perturbed frame
\widetilde{\text{\large{$\boldsymbol{\Psi}$}}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
. One will notice that this procedure is very different from Gram-Schmidt one.

By usual holomorphic functional calculus with the bounded self-adjoint operator
\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
, if we consider the function
\mathcal{z}\mapsto\mathcal{z}^{-1/2}=e^{-2^{-1}{\rm Ln}(z)}
that is holomorphic on any disk around
1
not containing
0
, we can define:

\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,:=\,\frac{i}{2\pi}\oint_{\mathscr{C}}d\mathcal{z}\,\mathcal{z}^{-1/2}\,\big(\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}-\mathcal{z}{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}\big)^{-1},

(4.17)

as a bounded self-adjoint operator commuting with
\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
. It satisfies the equalities:

P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,=\,\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,=\,[\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]^{2}\,\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}\,=\,\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}\,[\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]^{2}.

(4.18)

Lemma 4.14
.

The operator
\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
is a magnetic pseudo-differential operator with a symbol
\theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\in S^{-\infty}(\Xi)
and such that for
\widetilde{q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\in S^{-\infty}(\Xi)
the symbol of
\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
given by Proposition
4.7
and for any continuous semi-norm
{\nu}:S^{-\infty}(\Xi)_{\Gamma}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
there exists some constant
C_{{}_{\nu}}>0
such that:

{\nu}(\theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}-\widetilde{q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})\,\leq\,C_{{}_{\nu}}\epsilon,\hskip 11.74988pt\forall\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}].

Proof.

We have the identity

\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}=\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}\,\frac{i}{2\pi}\oint_{\mathscr{C}}d\mathcal{z}\,\mathcal{z}^{-3/2}\,\big(\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}-\mathcal{z}{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}\big)^{-1}.

Proposition
4.4
implies that
\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
is a magnetic pseudo-differential operator with symbol of class
S^{-\infty}
.
Via the magnetic Beals criterion we have that the above complex integral defines a magnetic pseudo-differential operator with a symbol in
S^{0}
, which by multiplication with
\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
becomes an operator with a symbol

\theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
of class
S^{-\infty}
.

Because
P_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}
is a projection, the arguments above imply that we may write:

\displaystyle\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}-P_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\,\frac{i}{2\pi}\oint_{\mathscr{C}}d\mathcal{z}\,\mathcal{z}^{-1/2}\,\Big(\big(\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}-\mathcal{z}{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}\big)^{-1}-\big(P_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}-\mathcal{z}{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}\big)^{-1}\Big)

(4.19)

\displaystyle=\,\frac{i}{2\pi}\oint_{\mathscr{C}}d\mathcal{z}\,\mathcal{z}^{-1/2}\,\big(\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}-\mathcal{z}{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}\big)^{-1}\big(P_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}-\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}\big)\big(P_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}-\mathcal{z}{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}\big)^{-1}

and by the same Beals & bootstrap argument we notice that the right hand side of (
4.19
) has a magnetic symbol in
\sigma^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\in{S}^{-\infty}(\Xi)
with semi-norms of order
\epsilon
, i.e. for any continuous semi-norm
\nu:S^{-\infty}(\Xi)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
there exists some
C_{\nu}>0
such that
\nu(\sigma^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})\leq{C}_{\nu}\,\epsilon
.

Coupling this with Lemma
4.11
we also get that
\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}-\widetilde{Q}_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}
has the same property.
∎

Proposition 4.15
.

For any
(\alpha,p,\epsilon)\in\Gamma\times\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}\times[0,\epsilon_{0}]
, the function
\psi^{\epsilon}_{\alpha,p}:=\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\,\psi_{p}\in{L^{2}}(\mathcal{X})
belongs to
\mathscr{S}(\mathcal{X})
with estimations uniform in
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
.

Proof.

From the construction in Subsection
3.4
we know that
\psi_{p}\in\mathscr{S}(\mathcal{X})
. For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
C_{n}>0
such that
|\psi_{p}(\hat{x}+\gamma)|\leq{C_{n}}<\gamma>^{-n}
. Since

\mathcal{X}\ni{x}\mapsto\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\gamma}(x)=\exp\Big(-i\epsilon\int_{[x,\gamma]}A^{\epsilon}\Big)\in\mathbb{U}(1)

is a smooth function of modulus 1, we have that
\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\,\psi_{p}\in\mathscr{S}(\mathcal{X})
and given any
n\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
C_{n}>0
such that
|\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\,\psi_{p}(\hat{x}+\gamma)|\leq{C_{n}}<\gamma-\alpha>^{-n}
. Finally our previous arguments implied that
\Theta_{\mathfrak{B}}^{\epsilon}
have integral kernels of class
\mathring{\mathscr{S}}_{\Delta}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})
with fast off-diagonal decay.
∎

Proposition 4.16
.

If we define for any
N\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}
the integral kernel:

\mathfrak{K}_{N}[P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]:=\underset{|\alpha|\leq\,N}{\sum}\ \underset{p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\big[\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\,\psi_{p}\big]\otimes\overline{\big[\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\,\psi_{p}\big]}\,,

and its associated integral operator
P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},N}:=\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\,\mathfrak{K}_{N}[P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]
, then the following limits exist and verify the equalities:

•

\mathfrak{K}=\underset{N\nearrow\infty}{\lim}\mathfrak{K}_{N}[P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]\in\mathring{\mathscr{S}}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})\bigcap{C}^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})
uniformly on compact sets in
\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}
;

•

\underset{N\nearrow\infty}{\lim}P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B},N}=Int\,\mathfrak{K}=P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\in\mathbb{B}\big(L^{2}(\mathcal{X})\big)
for the strong operator topology.

Proof.

Let us denote by
\mathfrak{K}[P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]_{\gamma}:=\underset{p\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\big[\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma}\,\psi_{p}\big]\otimes\overline{\big[\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma}\,\psi_{p}\big]}
and by
\mathfrak{K}[\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]\in\mathring{\mathscr{S}}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})
the integral kernel of
\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
. Then:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}[P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]_{\alpha}\diamond\mathfrak{K}[P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]_{\beta}=\underset{(p,q)\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}^{2}}{\sum}\big(\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\,\psi_{p}\,,\,\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\,\psi_{q}\big)_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}\big[\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\,\psi_{p}\big]\otimes\overline{\big[\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\,\psi_{q}\big]}

(4.20)

and considering each ’coefficient’:

\displaystyle\big(\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\,\psi_{p}\,,\,\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\,\psi_{q}\big)_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}dx\,dy\,dz\,\mathfrak{K}[\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}](x,y)\mathfrak{K}[\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}](x,z)\,\times

(4.21)

\displaystyle\times\,\overline{\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}(y)}\overline{\psi_{p}(y-\alpha)}\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(z)\psi_{q}(z-\beta)

(4.22)

and putting into evidence the decay of the functions under the integrals, we finally obtain the estimation:

\displaystyle<x-y>^{-n_{1}}<x-z>^{-n_{2}}<y-\alpha>^{-n_{3}}<z-\beta>^{-n_{4}}\leq

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\leq\,C<x-y>^{-n_{1}+n_{2}}<y-z>^{-n_{2}+n_{3}}<z-\alpha>^{-n_{3}+n_{4}}<\alpha-\beta>^{-n_{4}}\,,

that allows the use of the Cotlar-Stein Lemma.
∎

The previous arguments show that we can write:

\begin{split}\mathfrak{K}[P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}](x,y)&=\hskip-8.5359pt\underset{(\alpha,p)\in\Gamma\times\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\hskip-8.5359pt\big[\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\,\psi_{p}\big](x)\,\overline{\big[\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\,\psi_{p}\big](y)}.\end{split}

Proposition 4.17
.

The family

\text{\large{$\boldsymbol{\Psi}$}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}:=\big\{\psi^{\epsilon}_{\alpha,p}:=\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\,\psi_{p},\quad\forall(\alpha,p)\in\Gamma\times\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}\big\}

(4.23)

is a Parseval frame for the subspace
\mathfrak{L}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\subset\,L^{2}(\mathcal{X})
.

Proof.

Each
\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\,\psi_{p}
belongs to
\mathfrak{L}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}=\mathbb{R}{\rm ge}\,\,P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
.
If
f\in\mathfrak{L}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
, we have the equalities:

\displaystyle f=P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}f=\hskip-14.22636pt\underset{(\alpha,p)\in\Gamma\times\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\hskip-8.5359pt\big(\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\,\psi_{p}\,,\,f\big)_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}\,\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\,\psi_{p}.

∎

Remark 4.18
.

The abstract construction presented in the end of Appendix
C
implies the existence of an injective
C^{*}
-algebra morphism
\mathfrak{W}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}:\mathbb{B}\big(P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}L^{2}(\mathcal{X})\big)\rightarrow\mathbb{B}\big(\ell^{2}(\Gamma)\otimes{\mathbb{C}}^{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}\big)
associated with the Parseval frame
\text{\large{$\boldsymbol{\Psi}$}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
and given by the explicit formula:

\mathfrak{W}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}T\,=\,\mathfrak{C}_{\text{\large{$\boldsymbol{\Psi}$}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}}T\mathfrak{C}_{\text{\large{$\boldsymbol{\Psi}$}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}}^{*}.

Moreover, using the Parseval frame
\text{\large{$\boldsymbol{\Psi}$}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
in (
4.23
) we have the following “perturbed” version of the definition (
3.25
):

\big[\mathfrak{M}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}[T]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}\,:=\,\big({\cal{e}}_{\alpha}\otimes{\cal{e}}_{p}\,,\,\mathfrak{W}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}[T]\,{\cal{e}}_{\beta}\otimes{\cal{e}}_{q}\big)_{\ell^{2}(\Gamma)^{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}\,=\,\big(\psi^{\epsilon}_{\alpha,p}\,,\,T\,\psi^{\epsilon}_{\beta,q}\big)_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}.

(4.24)

Remark 4.19
.

We have the formula
P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}=\text{I}\hskip-2.0pt\text{I{\sf d}}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}}=\mathfrak{C}_{\text{\large{$\boldsymbol{\Psi}$}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}}\mathfrak{C}_{\text{\large{$\boldsymbol{\Psi}$}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}}^{*}
and taking
\epsilon=0
we obtain the formulas:

P_{\mathfrak{B}}\,=\,\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\,\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{B}},\quad\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{B}}:=\hskip-11.38092pt\underset{(\gamma,p)\in\Gamma\times\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}(\tau_{-\gamma}\psi_{p})\otimes(\overline{\tau_{-\gamma}\psi_{p})}\,.

4.4
The effective Hamiltonian of the isolated Bloch family in a regular magnetic field

Once we have put into evidence the orthogonal projection
P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
that is almost
H^{\epsilon}
-invariant, with an error of order
\epsilon
and have as Parseval frame the system
\text{\large{$\boldsymbol{\Psi}$}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
, let us consider the
“reduced magnetic Hamiltonian”
associated with the closed subspace
\mathfrak{L}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}=P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big[L^{2}(\mathcal{X})\big]
:

\mathfrak{H}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,:=\,P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,H^{\epsilon}\,P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}

(4.25)

and its associated infinite matrix
\mathfrak{M}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}[\mathfrak{H}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]
with respect to this Parseval frame as in (
4.24
).

Proposition 4.20
.

Suppose given a magnetic field as in (
2.10
) and a distribution kernel
\mathfrak{K}\in\mathring{\mathscr{S}}_{\Delta}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})
(see the Notation
2.1
) satisfying the periodicity property

(\tau_{\gamma}\otimes\tau_{\gamma})\mathfrak{K}=\mathfrak{K}\mbox{ for any }\gamma\in\Gamma\,.

The infinite matrix associated with the operator
T^{\epsilon}:=\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}\mathfrak{K}
with respect to the Parseval frame
\text{\large{$\boldsymbol{\Psi}$}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
in (
4.23
), is of the form

\displaystyle\big[\mathfrak{M}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}[T^{\epsilon}]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}:

\displaystyle=\big(\psi^{\epsilon}_{\alpha,p}\,,\,\big[\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}\mathfrak{K}\big]\psi^{\epsilon}_{\beta,q}\big)_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\Big[\big[[\mathring{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{B}}(\mathfrak{K})]_{\alpha-\beta}\big]_{p,q}+\epsilon\big[\widetilde{\mathfrak{M}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}[\mathfrak{K}]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}\Big]\,,

where:

•

\mathring{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{B}}(\mathfrak{K})\in{\cal{s}}(\Gamma;\mathscr{M}_{n_{\mathfrak{B}}})
is independent of
\epsilon
and is defined by

\big[[\mathring{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{B}}]_{\gamma}(\mathfrak{K})\big]_{p,q}:=\big(\psi_{p}\,,\,\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\big[(\tau_{\gamma}\otimes{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}})\mathfrak{K}\big]\psi_{q}\big)_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})},

for
\gamma\in\Gamma
and
1\leq p,q\leq n_{\mathfrak{B}}
;

•

the family

\big\{\widetilde{\mathfrak{M}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}[\mathfrak{K}],\,\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]\big\}
belongs to a bounded set of
\mathscr{M}^{\circ}_{\Gamma}[\mathscr{M}_{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}]
.

Proof.

Using (
4.23
) we notice that we have the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\big(\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\psi_{p}\,,\,[\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}\mathfrak{K}]\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\,\psi_{q}\big)_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}=\big\langle\,\mathfrak{K}\,,\,\big(\overline{\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}}\otimes\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\big)\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}\,\big(\overline{\tau_{-\alpha}\psi_{p}}\otimes\tau_{-\beta}\psi_{q}\big)\big\rangle_{\mathscr{S}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}+\,

(4.26)

\displaystyle+\,\big([\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}-{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}]\,\widetilde{\psi}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha,p}\,,\,[\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}\mathfrak{K}]\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,\widetilde{\psi}^{\epsilon}_{\beta,q}\big)_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}\,+\,\big(\widetilde{\psi}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha,p}\,,\,[\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}\mathfrak{K}][\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}-{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}]\,\widetilde{\psi}^{\epsilon}_{\beta,q}\big)_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}.

In order to deal with the first term in the r.h.s. of (
4.26
), we use Stokes’ formula in order to study the
\epsilon
dependence:

\displaystyle\big[\big(\overline{\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}}\otimes\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\big)\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}](x,y)

\displaystyle=\exp\Big[-i\Big(\int_{[\alpha,x]}A^{\epsilon}+\int_{[y,\beta]}A^{\epsilon}+\int_{[x,y]}A^{\epsilon}\Big]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\,\exp\Big[-i\Big(\int_{<\alpha,x,y>}B^{\epsilon}\Big)\Big]\,\exp\Big[-i\Big(\int_{<\alpha,y,\beta>}B^{\epsilon}\Big)\Big].

(4.27)

Using the computation in Subsection
B.3.2
we may conclude that

\displaystyle\Big|\big[\big(\overline{\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}}\otimes\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\big)\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}](x,y)\,-\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\Big|\leq\,C\epsilon\,\big[<x-\alpha><x-y>+<y-\beta><x-y>\big]\,,

(4.28)

and by iteration that all the derivatives of
\big(\overline{\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}}\otimes\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\big)\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}
may be bounded by polynomials of the form:

\epsilon^{M}<x-\alpha>^{M_{1}}<y-\beta>^{M_{2}}<x-y>^{M_{3}}.

Denoting
\mathfrak{k}_{N}(x,y):=<x-y>^{N}
and
\mathfrak{K}^{\prime}_{N}:=\mathfrak{k}_{N}\mathfrak{K}\in\mathring{\mathscr{S}}_{\Delta}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})
we notice that:

\displaystyle\big\langle\,\mathfrak{K}\,,\,\big(\overline{\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}}\otimes\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\big)\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}\,\big(\overline{\tau_{-\alpha}\psi_{p}}\otimes\tau_{-\beta}\psi_{q}\big)\big\rangle_{\mathscr{S}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}=\big\langle\,\mathfrak{K}\,,\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\,\big(\overline{\tau_{-\alpha}\psi_{p}}\otimes\tau_{-\beta}\psi_{q}\big)\big\rangle_{\mathscr{S}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}\,+

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\big\langle\,\mathfrak{K}^{\prime}_{N}\,,\,\mathfrak{k}_{-N}\Big[\big[\big(\overline{\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}}\otimes\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\big)\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}]\,-\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\Big]\big(\overline{\tau_{-\alpha}\psi_{p}}\otimes\tau_{-\beta}\psi_{q}\big)\big\rangle_{\mathscr{S}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}.

(4.29)

Taking now into account the estimation (
4.28
) and the following remarks concerning its derivatives and noticing that:

\displaystyle<x-y>^{-N}=<(x-\alpha)-(y-\beta)+(\alpha-\beta)>^{-N}\leq\,C_{N}<x-\alpha>^{N}<y-\beta>^{N}<\alpha-\beta>^{-N}

we conclude that, for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
\Phi_{N,\alpha,\beta}\in\mathscr{S}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})
uniformly in
(\alpha,\beta)\in\Gamma\times\Gamma
such that:

\displaystyle\Big[\mathfrak{k}_{-N}\Big[\big[\big(\overline{\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}}\otimes\Lambda^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\big)\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}]\,-\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\Big]\big(\overline{\tau_{-\alpha}\psi_{p}}\otimes\tau_{-\beta}\psi_{q}\big)\Big](x,y)=\epsilon\,<\alpha-\beta>^{-N}\Phi_{N,\alpha,\beta}(x,y).

Moreover, we may obtain an expansion in powers of
\epsilon
convergent in matrix norm for
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
for some small enough
\epsilon_{0}>0
, where the main contribution to (
4.29
) is given by:

\displaystyle\big\langle\,\mathfrak{K}\,,\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\,\big(\overline{\tau_{-\alpha}\psi_{p}}\otimes\tau_{-\beta}\psi_{q}\big)\big\rangle_{\mathscr{S}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\,\big((\tau_{-\alpha}\psi_{p})\,,\,(\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\,\mathfrak{K})\,(\tau_{-\beta}\psi_{q})\big)_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})},

the remainder being one of the terms in the sum of contributions representing
\big[\widetilde{\mathfrak{M}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}[\mathfrak{K}]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}
.
Now let us deal with the two remainder terms in (
4.26
) and notice that we have to control the following type of norms:

\big\|[\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}-{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}]\,\widetilde{\psi}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha,p}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}
, for
(\alpha,p)\in\Gamma\times\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}
.
We shall do that by treating separately the two differences:

\big\|[\Theta^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}-\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]\,\widetilde{\psi}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha,p}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})},\qquad\big\|[\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}-{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}]\,\widetilde{\psi}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha,p}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}.

(4.30)

For the first difference we use Lemma
4.14
, while
for the second norm in (
4.30
) let us start from Proposition
4.5
and compute using (
4.7
):

\displaystyle\big[[\widetilde{Q}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}-{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}]\,\widetilde{\psi}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha,p}\big](x)

\displaystyle=\underset{\gamma\in\Gamma,\,q\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\hskip-8.5359pt\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma}\psi_{q}(x)\,\int_{\mathbb{Z}}dz\,\overline{\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma}\psi_{q}(z)}\,\big(\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\psi_{p}(z)\big)\,-\,\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\psi_{p}(x)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\hskip-8.5359pt\underset{\gamma\neq\alpha,\,q\in\underline{n_{\mathfrak{B}}}}{\sum}\hskip-8.5359pt\big[[\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}\,\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma}\psi_{q}(x).

Appendix
B.3.1
may be used to obtain precise estimations and an
\epsilon
-expansion for the remainder
\big[[\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}
. ∎

The infinite matrix of the effective Hamiltonian.

Let us study the matrix of the
“reduced magnetic Hamiltonian”

\mathfrak{M}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}[\mathfrak{H}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]
, using Proposition
4.20
, formula (
3.26
) and the equality:

\displaystyle\big((\tau_{-\alpha}\psi_{p})\,,\,\big[\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\,\mathfrak{K}[h^{\circ}]\big]\,(\tau_{-\beta}\psi_{q})\big)_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}=\big((\tau_{-\alpha}\psi_{p})\,,\,\big[\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\,\mathfrak{K}[h_{\mathfrak{B}}]\big]\,(\tau_{-\beta}\psi_{q})\big)_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}.

We get

\displaystyle\big[\mathfrak{M}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}[\mathfrak{H}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\psi_{p}\,,\,H^{\epsilon}\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\,\psi_{q}\big)_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}=\big(\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\alpha}\psi_{p}\,,\,\big[\mathfrak{I}\mathit{nt}\,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}\mathfrak{K}[h^{\circ}]\big]\mathfrak{T}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}\,\psi_{q}\big)_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}

(4.31)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\Big[\big[\mathring{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{B}}[H_{\mathfrak{B}}]_{\alpha-\beta}\big]_{p,q}+\epsilon\big[\widetilde{\mathfrak{M}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big[\mathfrak{K}[h^{\circ}]\big]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}\Big]\,,

with
\quad\widetilde{\mathfrak{M}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big[\mathfrak{K}[h^{\circ}]\big]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}=
:

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{-1}\Lambda^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\Big\langle\,\mathfrak{K}[h^{\circ}]\,,\,\Big[1-\Omega^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\cdot,\cdot)\big(1\otimes\Omega^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta,\cdot)\big)\Big]\,\big(\overline{\tau_{-\alpha}\psi_{p}}\otimes\tau_{-\beta}\psi_{q}\big)\Big\rangle_{\mathscr{S}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}

\displaystyle\ =-i\Big\langle\,\mathfrak{K}[h^{\circ}]\,,\,\Big[\Big(\int_{<\alpha,\cdot,\cdot>}\,B^{\epsilon}\Big)+\Big(1\otimes\int_{<\alpha,\beta,\cdot>}\,B^{\epsilon}\Big)\Big]\big(\overline{\tau_{-\alpha}\psi_{p}}\otimes\tau_{-\beta}\psi_{q}\big)\Big\rangle_{\mathscr{S}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}+\,\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle\qquad\,=:\,\big[\mathfrak{m}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big[\mathfrak{K}[h^{\circ}]\big]_{\alpha,\beta}\big]_{p,q}\,+\,\mathcal{O}(\epsilon).

(4.32)

with
\mathfrak{m}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}[\mathfrak{K}[h^{\circ}]]
given explicitely in Formula (
B.15
).

We have thus obtained the proof of points
1
and
2
in Theorem
2.18
.

4.5
Connection with the Peierls-Onsager substitution procedure.

Let us very briefly summarize the main results of this section, giving an informal short version of the statement 2 in Theorem
2.18
.

Given a
\Gamma
-periodic pseudo-differential operator with an isolated Bloch family
\mathfrak{B}
, the dynamics of the states in the subspace associated with the family
\mathfrak{B}
is given by an infinite Toeplitz matrix
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}[H_{\mathfrak{B}}]
(see (
3.26
)) defined by a rapidly decaying
\Gamma
-indexed sequence
\mathring{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{B}}[H_{\mathfrak{B}}]
(see (
3.27
)) (i.e.
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}[H_{\mathfrak{B}}]_{\alpha,\beta}=\mathring{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{B}}[H_{\mathfrak{B}}]_{\alpha-\beta}\in\mathscr{M}(n_{\mathfrak{B}})
). Superposing a weak, regular long-range magnetic field, one can still isolate a ”quasi-invariant” subspace
\mathfrak{L}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
(Definition
4.9
), with an error of the order of magnitude of the magnetic field, and an
’effective Hamiltonian”
approximating the real dynamics and given by an infinite
\Gamma
-indexed matrix. Let us emphasize here that the singular part of this approximation, coming from the long-range character of the magnetic field, is treated as a magnetic quantization of the free matrix valued symbol
\mathring{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{B}}[H_{\mathfrak{B}}]
in the spirit of the Peierls-Onsager procedure, isolating also a ’regular’ part that is small in norm of the order of magnitude of the magnetic field (see (
4.31
) and (
4.32
)):

\mathfrak{M}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}[\mathfrak{H}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}]_{\alpha,\beta}=\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\mathring{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{B}}[H_{\mathfrak{B}}]_{\alpha-\beta}\,+\,\epsilon\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big[\mathfrak{K}[h^{\circ}]\big]_{\alpha,\beta}\,+\,\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,,

(4.33)

(where we have denoted by
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\Gamma}
the restriction of
\widetilde{\Lambda}:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathbb{S}^{1}
to
\Gamma\times\Gamma
). In fact, the ’singular part’ in (
4.33
) may be written as:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\mathring{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{B}}[H_{\mathfrak{B}}]_{\alpha-\beta}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}(\alpha,\beta)\underset{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\sum}[\mathring{U}(-\gamma)]_{\alpha,\beta}\otimes\big(\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}_{*}}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{B}}[\widehat{H}_{\mathfrak{B}}]\big)(\gamma)

(4.34)

\displaystyle=\underset{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\sum}\big[\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}\,\mathring{U}(-\gamma)\big]_{\alpha,\beta}\otimes\big(\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}_{*}}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{B}}[\widehat{H}_{\mathfrak{B}}](\gamma)\,.

(4.35)

This may be considered as the matrix of the following operator in
\ell^{2}(\Gamma)\otimes{\mathbb{C}}^{\mathfrak{B}}
:

\widetilde{\mathfrak{Op}}^{\epsilon}\big(\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{B}}[\widehat{H}_{\mathfrak{B}}]\big)\,:=\,\underset{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\sum}\big[\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}\,\mathring{U}(-\gamma)\big]\otimes\big(\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}_{*}}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{B}}[\widehat{H}_{\mathfrak{B}}]\big)(\gamma)\,,

(4.36)

that we may compare with the unperturbed one in (
3.29
).

5
Dynamics of the perturbed isolated Bloch family.

Finally we proceed to compare the real perturbed Hamiltonian
H^{\epsilon}
(see (
2.14
)) with the
“magnetic perturbation of the isolated Bloch family Hamiltonian”
(
4.25
).

5.1
Reduction to the subspace
{P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}

5.1.1
Main statement

This subsection is devoted to the proof of point
2
i
in Theorem
2.18
that is contained in the following statement:

Theorem 5.1
.

Given a Hamiltonian
H^{\circ}
as in (
2.7
) satisfying Hypotheses
2.10
, a magnetic field as in (
2.10
) and the perturbed Hamiltonian
H^{\epsilon}
as in (
2.14
), there exists
\delta_{0}>0
such that for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}]
, if we denote by
J^{\delta}_{\mathfrak{B}}:=\big(E_{-}+{2}\delta\,,\,E_{+}-{2}\delta\big)
, there exist constants
\epsilon_{0}>0
and
C>0
such that, for any
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
,
there exists an orthogonal projection
P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
and an effective magnetic Hamiltonian
\mathfrak{H}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}:=P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,H^{\epsilon}\,P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
commuting with
P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
, satisfying:

1.

For any
\lambda\in J^{\delta}_{\mathfrak{B}}
the operator
({\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}-P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})(H^{\epsilon}-\lambda{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}})({\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}-P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})
is invertible in the subspace
({\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}-P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})L^{2}(\mathcal{X})
and its inverse denoted by
[R^{\bot}_{\epsilon}(\lambda)]
is norm-bounded in
\mathbb{B}\big(({\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}-P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})L^{2}(\mathcal{X})\big)
for
\lambda
in any compact subinterval
J\subset\,J^{\delta}_{\mathfrak{B}}
.

2.

We have the equality:

J\cap\sigma(H^{\epsilon})=J\cap\sigma\Big(\big\{\mathfrak{H}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,-\,P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}H^{\epsilon}[R^{\bot}_{\epsilon,\mathcal{c}}(\lambda)]H^{\epsilon}P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big\}\big|_{P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}\Big)\,.

3.

For any
\lambda\in\,J^{\delta}_{\mathfrak{B}}\setminus\sigma(H^{\epsilon})
, considering the orthogonal decomposition

L^{2}(\mathcal{X})=P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,L^{2}(\mathcal{X})\,\oplus\,({\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}-P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})L^{2}(\mathcal{X})

and denoting by
[R^{\sim}_{\epsilon}(\lambda)]
the inverse in
P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,L^{2}(\mathcal{X})
of the operator

\mathfrak{H}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,-\,P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}H^{\epsilon}[R^{\bot}_{\epsilon}(\lambda)]H^{\epsilon}P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\,-\,\lambda\,P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}

we have the Feshbach-Schur block decomposition:

\displaystyle\big(H^{\epsilon}-\lambda{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}\big)^{-1}

\displaystyle\qquad=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}[R^{\sim}_{\epsilon}(\lambda)]&-[R^{\sim}_{\epsilon}(\lambda)]H^{\epsilon}[R^{\bot}_{\epsilon}(\lambda)]\\
-[R^{\bot}_{\epsilon}(\lambda)]H^{\epsilon}[R^{\sim}_{\epsilon}(\lambda)]&[R^{\bot}_{\epsilon}(\lambda)]+[R^{\bot}_{\epsilon}H^{\epsilon}[R^{\sim}_{\epsilon}(\lambda)]H^{\epsilon}[R^{\bot}_{\epsilon}(\lambda)]\end{array}\right)

and the estimate:

\big\|P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}H^{\epsilon}[R^{\bot}_{\epsilon}(\lambda)]H^{\epsilon}P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big\|_{\mathbb{B}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))}\,\leq\,C\epsilon^{2}\,\big\|R^{\bot}_{\epsilon}(\lambda)\big\|_{\mathbb{B}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))}.

(5.1)

Remark 5.2
.

We can extend the estimate (
5.1
) and point 3 of the above theorem to any complex number
\lambda
in
\{\mathcal{z}\in\mathbb{C}\,,\,\Re\hskip-1.0pt{\cal{e}}\mathcal{z}\in\mathring{J}_{\mathfrak{B}}\}
, with uniform estimates with respect to
\Im\hskip-1.0pt\mathcal{m}\,\lambda
.

The main ingredient in the proof of Theorem
2.18
is the abstract spectral result presented in Paragraph
5.1.2
(that we also used in
[
14
,
13
]
), together with the magnetic pseudodifferential calculus. We choose
P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
as in (
4.9
) and
\mathfrak{H}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
as in (
4.25
).

5.1.2
Schur complement and reduction to a quasi-invariant subspace.

We shall consider the following abstract setting already introduced and used in
[
14
,
15
]
.

Hypothesis 5.3
.

In a separable complex Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
we consider a family of pairs
(H_{\kappa},P_{\kappa})
indexed by
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{0}]
for some
\kappa_{0}>0
, where
H_{\kappa}:\mathcal{D}(H_{\kappa})\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
is a lower-semibounded self-adjoint operator and
\Pi_{\kappa}=\Pi_{\kappa}^{*}=\Pi_{\kappa}^{2}
is an orthogonal projection, such that, for
\Pi_{\kappa}^{\bot}:={\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}-\Pi_{\kappa}
, we have the properties:

1.

there exists
C>0
such that for any
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{0}]
we have that
\Pi_{\kappa}\mathcal{H}\subset\mathcal{D}(H_{\kappa})
and
\|\Pi_{\kappa}^{\bot}H_{\kappa}\Pi_{\kappa}\|_{\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{H})}\,\leq\,C\,\kappa
;

2.

there exists an interval
J\subset\mathbb{R}
with non-void interior, such that for any
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{0}]
and any
t\in J
the operator
\Pi_{\kappa}^{\bot}H_{\kappa}\Pi_{\kappa}^{\bot}\,-\,t\Pi_{\kappa}^{\bot}
is invertible as operator in
\Pi_{\kappa}^{\bot}\mathcal{H}
with the inverse being uniformly bounded on
J
.

We notice that under Hypothesis
5.3
we have the identity:

\Pi_{\kappa}H_{\kappa}R^{\bot}_{\kappa}(t)H_{\kappa}\Pi_{\kappa}=\Pi_{\kappa}H_{\kappa}\Pi_{\kappa}^{\bot}R^{\bot}_{\kappa}(t)\Pi_{\kappa}^{\bot}H\Pi_{\kappa}\,\in\,\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{H})

and the estimate (for some
C>0
):

\big\|\Pi_{\kappa}H_{\kappa}R^{\bot}_{\kappa}(t)H_{\kappa}\Pi_{\kappa}\big\|_{\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{H})}\,\leq\,C\kappa^{2}\,\big\|R^{\bot}_{\kappa}(t)\big\|_{\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{H})}.

(5.2)

A simple algebraic computation allows us to prove the following statement about the Schur complement (
[
26
]
).

Proposition 5.4
.

Under Hypothesis
5.3
we have that:

•

t\in J\cap\sigma(H)
if and only if
t\in J\cap\sigma\big(\Pi_{\kappa}H_{\kappa}\Pi_{\kappa}\,-\,\Pi_{\kappa}HR^{\bot}_{\kappa}(t)H_{\kappa}\Pi_{\kappa}\big)
;

•

if we denote by
R^{\sim}_{\kappa}(t):=\big(\Pi_{\kappa}(H_{\kappa}-t)\Pi_{\kappa}\,-\,\Pi_{\kappa}H_{\kappa}R^{\bot}_{\kappa}(t)H_{\kappa}\Pi_{\kappa}\big)^{-1}
as operator in
\Pi_{\kappa}\mathcal{H}
, we have the identity

\begin{split}\big(H_{\kappa}-t{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}_{\mathcal{H}}\big)^{-1}&=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\Pi_{\kappa}(H_{\kappa}-t)\Pi_{\kappa}&\Pi_{\kappa}H_{\kappa}\Pi_{\kappa}^{\bot}\\
\Pi_{\kappa}^{\bot}H_{\kappa}\Pi_{\kappa}&\Pi_{\kappa}^{\bot}(H_{\kappa}-t)\Pi_{\kappa}^{\bot}\end{array}\right)^{-1}\\
&=\ \left(\begin{array}[]{cc}R^{\sim}_{\kappa}(t)&-R^{\sim}_{\kappa}(t)H_{\kappa}R^{\bot}_{\kappa}(t)\\
-R^{\bot}_{\kappa}(t)HR^{\sim}_{\kappa}(t)&R^{\bot}_{\kappa}(t)+R^{\bot}_{\kappa}(t)H_{\kappa}R^{\sim}_{\kappa}(t)H_{\kappa}R^{\bot}_{\kappa}(t)\end{array}\right).\end{split}

Corollary 5.5
.

Under Hypothesis
5.3
, the operator
\mathfrak{H}_{\kappa}:=\Pi_{\kappa}H_{\kappa}\Pi_{\kappa}
defines a bounded self-adjoint operator acting in
\Pi_{\kappa}\mathcal{H}
and there exists
C>0
such that, for all
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{0}]
,

\max\left\{\underset{\lambda\in J\cap\sigma(H_{\kappa})}{\sup}{\rm dist}\Big(\lambda\,,\,\sigma\big(\mathfrak{H}_{\kappa}\big)\Big)\ ,\ \underset{\lambda\in J\cap\sigma(\mathfrak{H}_{\kappa})}{\sup}{\rm dist}\Big(\lambda\,,\,\sigma(H_{\kappa})\Big)\right\}\,\leq\,C\,\kappa^{2}\,\big\|R^{\bot}_{\kappa}(t)\big\|_{\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{H})}.

5.1.3
Verification of Hypothesis
5.3
and end of the proof of Theorem
2.18

Let us recall the definitions and notations in
3.1
,
3.5
and
4.1
and using the context of Paragraph
5.1.2
, let us take
\mathcal{H}=L^{2}(\mathcal{X})
,
\kappa=\epsilon
,
\Pi_{\kappa}=P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
and
H_{\kappa}=H^{\epsilon}
for
P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}
as in (
4.9
), for any
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
.

Verification of condition 1.

The previous Propositions
4.15
and
4.16
clearly imply that
P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}L^{2}(\mathcal{X})\subset\mathcal{D}(H^{\epsilon})
while Formula (
4.15
) clearly implies that:

({\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}-P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})H^{\epsilon}{P}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}=({\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}-P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})\big[H^{\epsilon}\,,\,P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big]=\epsilon({\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}-P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})\,\mathfrak{X}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,,

and thus the desired estimation in the first condition of Hypothesis
5.3
.

Verification of condition 2.

Let us consider the product
P^{\epsilon}_{\bot}H^{\epsilon}P^{\epsilon}_{\bot}
. Using Remark
3.12
we notice that
H_{\bot}=P_{\bot}HP_{\bot}
has two spectral gaps:
[0,E_{0}]
and
[E_{-},E_{+}]
, with
0<E_{0}\leq E_{-}<E_{+}<\infty
. We shall
prove that condition 2 in Hypothesis
5.3
is verified for some interval
J\subset[E_{-},E_{+}]
with non-void interior and the pair
\big(H^{\epsilon}\,,\,P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big)
in
\mathcal{H}=L^{2}(\mathcal{X})
where we consider, as explained above,
\kappa=\epsilon
. Then, let us introduce:

P^{\epsilon}_{\bot}:={\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}-P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}

and study the difference:

\displaystyle P^{\epsilon}_{\bot}H^{\epsilon}P^{\epsilon}_{\bot}\,-\,H^{\epsilon}_{\bot}\,

\displaystyle=\,H^{\epsilon}-H^{\epsilon}_{\bot}+P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}H^{\epsilon}{P}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}-\big(P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}H^{\epsilon}+H^{\epsilon}{P}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big)

\displaystyle=\,H^{\epsilon}-H^{\epsilon}_{\bot}+P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}H^{\epsilon}{P}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}+\big[H^{\epsilon}\,,\,P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big]-2H^{\epsilon}{P}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}

\displaystyle=\,H^{\epsilon}-H^{\epsilon}_{\bot}-P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}H^{\epsilon}{P}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}+\big[H^{\epsilon}\,,\,P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big]-2P^{\epsilon}_{\bot}{H}^{\epsilon}{P}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}

\displaystyle=\,H^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}-P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}H^{\epsilon}{P}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}-\epsilon\big({\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}+2P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big)\mathfrak{X}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2}).

Then we notice that:

\displaystyle H^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}-P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}H^{\epsilon}{P}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}\big(h_{\mathfrak{B}}-p^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\sharp^{\epsilon}{h}\sharp^{\epsilon}{p}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big)=\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}\big(p_{\mathfrak{B}}\sharp^{\circ}{h}\sharp^{\circ}{p}_{\mathfrak{B}}-p^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\sharp^{\epsilon}{h}\sharp^{\epsilon}{p}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}\big(p_{\mathfrak{B}}\sharp^{\circ}{h}\sharp^{\circ}{p}_{\mathfrak{B}}-p_{\mathfrak{B}}\sharp^{\epsilon}{h}\sharp^{\epsilon}\mathfrak{p}_{\mathfrak{B}}+p_{\mathfrak{B}}\sharp^{\epsilon}{h}\sharp^{\epsilon}{p}_{\mathfrak{B}}-p^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\sharp^{\epsilon}{h}\sharp^{\epsilon}{p}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}\big((p_{\mathfrak{B}}\sharp^{\circ}{h})\sharp^{\circ}{p}_{\mathfrak{B}}-(p_{\mathfrak{B}}\sharp^{\circ}{h})\sharp^{\epsilon}{p}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big)+\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}\big((p_{\mathfrak{B}}\sharp^{\circ}{h}-p_{\mathfrak{B}}\sharp^{\epsilon}{h})\sharp^{\epsilon}{p}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}\big((p_{\mathfrak{B}}-p^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})\sharp^{\epsilon}{h}\sharp^{\epsilon}{p}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}\big)+\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}\big({p}_{\mathfrak{B}}\sharp^{\epsilon}{h}\sharp^{\epsilon}(p_{\mathfrak{B}}-p^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})\big)

and using also Proposition
B.12
we conclude that:

\begin{split}&H^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}-P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}H^{\epsilon}{P}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}=\\
&\ =\epsilon\mathfrak{Op}^{\epsilon}\big(\mathcal{r}_{\epsilon}(p_{\mathfrak{B}},h_{\mathfrak{B}})+\mathcal{r}_{\epsilon}(h,p_{\mathfrak{B}})\sharp^{\circ}{p}_{\mathfrak{B}}+(p_{\mathfrak{B}}-p^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})\sharp^{\circ}p_{\mathfrak{B}}+p_{\mathfrak{B}}\sharp^{\circ}(p_{\mathfrak{B}}-p^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})\big)\,+\,\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,.\end{split}

(5.3)

The arguments in Subsection
3.1
imply that:
\sigma\big(H_{\bot}\big)\subset\{0\}\cup[E_{0},E_{-}]\cup[E_{+},+\infty)
. Thus we can use the last inclusion listed in (
4.2
) in order to conclude that there exists
\delta_{0}>0
such that for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}]
there exist constants
\epsilon_{0}(\delta)>0
and
C>0
such that for any
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
:

J:=\big(E_{-}+\delta\,,\,E_{+}-\delta\big)\subset\,\mathbb{R}\setminus\sigma\big(H^{\epsilon}_{\bot}\big).

Thus we have proved the following statement:

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
H^{\circ}
in (
2.7
) satisfy Hypothesis
2.10
and a perturbation by a magnetic field verifying (
2.10
). With
J_{\mathfrak{B}}:=[E_{-},E_{+}]\subset\mathbb{R}
as in Hypothesis
2.10
, there exists
\delta_{0}>0
such that for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}]
the interval
J^{\delta}_{\mathfrak{B}}:=\big(E_{-}+\delta\,,\,E_{+}-\delta\big)
is not void and there exists a constant
\epsilon_{0}(\delta)>0
such that for any
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
and for any
t\in J^{\delta}_{\mathfrak{B}}
the operator
({\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}-P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})\big(H^{\epsilon}_{\bot}\,-\,t{\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}\big)({\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}-P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})
is invertible as a self-adjoint operator in
({\rm{1}\hskip-3.0pt\mathbf{l}}-P^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}})L^{2}(\mathcal{X})
. This inverse is denoted by
[R^{\bot}_{\epsilon}(t)]
and is uniformly bounded on
J^{\delta}_{\mathfrak{B}}
.

Putting this result together with (
5.3
), we obtain that the Condition 2 in Hypothesis
5.3
is satisfied, and Theorem
2.18
is then a direct consequence of Proposition
5.4
and Corollary
5.5
.

5.2
The effective time evolution

The proof of point
2
ii
in Theorem
2.18
will follow from the following theorem.

Theorem 5.7
.

Under the hypotheses of Theorem
2.18
, given any compact interval
J\subset J^{\delta}_{\mathfrak{B}}
(with
J^{\delta}_{\mathfrak{B}}
as in the Theorem
2.18
), if we denote by
E_{J}(H^{\epsilon})
the spectral projection of
H^{\epsilon}
for the interval
J\subset\mathbb{R}
, there exist
C>0
and
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that for any
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
and for all
v
in the range of
E_{J}(H^{\epsilon})
, we have the estimation:

\big\|e^{-itH^{\epsilon}}v-e^{-it\mathfrak{H}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{B}}}v\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}\,\leq\,C\,\Big[\epsilon\,+\,{(1+|t|)^{3}}\,\epsilon^{2}\Big]\,\|v\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})},\ \forall t\in\mathbb{R}\,.

(5.4)

Proof.

In order to prove (
5.4
), we consider states
v
with energies in a compact interval
J\subset J_{\mathfrak{B}}
